I just know they're tracking my buys and messing with those launch numbers to make me look stupid. It just feels too real to not be true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your feeling of being tracked is not real, even for a moment?\n\nPatient: I mean... maybe for a split second when my supervisor first put those reports in front of me. I just know something's going on. That's mainly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your concerns about people tracking your purchases might not be true?\n\nPatient: I mean... I know I just brought this up, but even with my supervisor showing me those reports and telling me there's no proof... I just can't shake the feeling. \n\nI guess I understand why you'd ask that, since the paperwork doesn't officially show anything. But... no, I really don't think I'm wrong. I just know they're tracking my buys and messing with those launch numbers to make me look stupid. It just feels too real to not be true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your feeling of being tracked is not real, even for a moment?\n\nPatient: I mean... maybe for a split second when my supervisor first put those reports in front of me. You want to believe the paperwork. But... no, not really. \n\nLike I was saying, that's what really made me come in today. I just can't shake the feeling that the people at work, and those vendors, are tracking my buys. They're messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Even when my supervisor shows me the reports don't prove it, I just know it's happening.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like people are tracking your purchases and messing with the launch numbers?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on for a while. The whole thing with people tracking me and messing with the numbers... and, well, hearing that voice I haven't mentioned yet... it's all been happening for roughly ten months now.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voice you mentioned, what does it say?\n\nPatient: Well, it's a man's voice. I usually hear it when I'm alone, like if I'm in the stockroom at work or driving back from a vendor meeting. It sounds like it's coming from right behind me. He'll say things like, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he'll just laugh at the picks I made for the store. But every time I turn around to see who's there, nobody's around.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something specific, like hurt yourself or someone else?\n\nPatient: No, no, he doesn't say anything like that. He doesn't tell me to hurt anybody or myself or anything crazy. It's just... mostly him laughing at me or telling me to hurry up. That's really it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other unusual thoughts or experiences besides the voice and the feeling of being tracked?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um... it's really just the voice and the tracking stuff I told you about. I haven't noticed anything else weird going on besides that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely energetic or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um... I get these stretches where I feel like the whole city is just opening up for me, like I have all this energy. But then, if someone at work questions a purchase order I put in, or if they tell me to slow down, I just... I snap way harder than I mean to. So yeah, I definitely get really irritable during those times, too.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these feelings of extreme energy and irritability all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Um... they do come and go, but honestly, looking back on it, most of the time since all this started has been taken up by those wired stretches. Like, I'll just be overworking, feeling super confident about everything, and it's really hard for me to slow down. I do get these shorter, calmer periods in between, but the energetic and irritable times definitely take up the majority of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or feel like your thoughts are disjointed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, my thoughts can race when I'm in one of those energetic moods, but I don't really have trouble getting my point across. People seem to understand what I'm saying just fine, and I don't feel like my thoughts are all over the place or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where the voice stopped or the feeling of being tracked went away for more than a few days?\n\nPatient: No, they don't really stop. Like, even when I had a calmer stretch a while back... I was sleeping more normally, and I didn't feel like I was running the whole market or anything. But even then, for a few weeks, I still believed people were tracking my buys. And I was still hearing that voice when I was alone in the stockroom. So, no, it doesn't really go away.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your periods of extreme energy and irritability have ever lasted for at least a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. When I get like that, it usually lasts for at least a week, sometimes a lot longer. It's not just a day or two of feeling wired; it's a whole stretch where I'm just going and going.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling extremely energetic, do you still believe people are tracking your purchases and messing with the launch numbers?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, it's all happening at the same time. Like, during those weeks when I'm barely sleeping and just talking nonstop about sneaker trends, feeling totally untouchable at work... I'm still completely convinced the vendors are manipulating the numbers. And I still hear that voice, too. When I'm that wired, it's usually pushing me to move faster, telling me to keep up. So yeah, the tracking and the voice are still there even when I have all that energy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear or your beliefs about being tracked are stronger when you're in one of those energetic and irritable periods?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they're stronger, to be honest. I haven't really noticed a difference like that. Like I said, they're just always there. \n\nI guess when I'm super energetic, I might react to the voice more, or I'm just moving faster because of it, but the actual belief that I'm being tracked or the voice itself... I don't think it gets louder or more intense. It's just kind of a constant thing, no matter how much energy I have. I haven't really paid attention to whether my mood changes how strong it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your belief about people tracking your purchases might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not really. That's actually the main reason I ended up coming in here. I just can't shake the feeling that people at work, and even some of the vendors, are tracking my buys. I know they're messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. My supervisor has sat down with me and shown me the reports, and he says they don't prove anything is happening, but... It just feels too real to not be true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your feeling of being tracked is not real, even for a moment?\n\nPatient: I mean... maybe for a split second when my supervisor first put those reports in front of me. You want to believe the paperwork. But... no, not really. \n\nLike I was saying, that's what really made me come in today. I just can't shake the feeling that the people at work, and those vendors, are tracking my buys. They're messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Even when my supervisor shows me the reports don't prove it, I just know it's happening.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like people are tracking your purchases and messing with the launch numbers?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on for a while. The whole thing with people tracking me and messing with the numbers... and, well, hearing that voice I haven't mentioned yet... it's all been happening for roughly ten months now.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voice you mentioned, what does it say?\n\nPatient: Well, it's a man's voice. I usually hear it when I'm alone, like if I'm in the stockroom at work or driving back from a vendor meeting. It sounds like it's coming from right behind me. He'll say things like, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he'll just laugh at the picks I made for the store. But every time I turn around to see who's there, nobody's around.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something specific, like hurt yourself or someone else?\n\nPatient: No, no, he doesn't say anything like that. He doesn't tell me to hurt anybody or myself or anything crazy. It's just... mostly him laughing at me or telling me to hurry up. That's really it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other unusual thoughts or experiences besides the voice and the feeling of being tracked?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um... it's really just the voice and the tracking stuff I told you about. I haven't noticed anything else weird going on besides that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely energetic or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um... I get these stretches where I feel like the whole city is just opening up for me, like I have all this energy. But then, if someone at work questions a purchase order I put in, or if they tell me to slow down, I just... I snap way harder than I mean to. So yeah, I definitely get really irritable during those times, too.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these feelings of extreme energy and irritability all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Um... they do come and go, but honestly, looking back on it, most of the time since all this started has been taken up by those wired stretches. Like, I'll just be overworking, feeling super confident about everything, and it's really hard for me to slow down. I do get these shorter, calmer periods in between, but the energetic and irritable times definitely take up the majority of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or feel like your thoughts are disjointed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, my thoughts can race when I'm in one of those energetic moods, but I don't really have trouble getting my point across. People seem to understand what I'm saying just fine, and I don't feel like my thoughts are all over the place or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where the voice stopped or the feeling of being tracked went away for more than a few days?\n\nPatient: No, they don't really stop. Like, even when I had a calmer stretch a while back... I was sleeping more normally, and I didn't feel like I was running the whole market or anything. But even then, for a few weeks, I still believed people were tracking my buys. And I was still hearing that voice when I was alone in the stockroom. So, no, it doesn't really go away.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your periods of extreme energy and irritability have ever lasted for at least a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. When I get like that, it usually lasts for at least a week, sometimes a lot longer. It's not just a day or two of feeling wired; it's a whole stretch where I'm just going and going.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling extremely energetic, do you still believe people are tracking your purchases and messing with the launch numbers?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, it's all happening at the same time. Like, during those weeks when I'm barely sleeping and just talking nonstop about sneaker trends, feeling totally untouchable at work... I'm still completely convinced the vendors are manipulating the numbers. And I still hear that voice, too. When I'm that wired, it's usually pushing me to move faster, telling me to keep up. So yeah, the tracking and the voice are still there even when I have all that energy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear or your beliefs about being tracked are stronger when you're in one of those energetic and irritable periods?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they're stronger, to be honest. I haven't really noticed a difference like that. Like I said, they're just always there. \n\nI guess when I'm super energetic, I might react to the voice more, or I'm just moving faster because of it, but the actual belief that I'm being tracked or the voice itself... I don't think it gets louder or more intense. It's just kind of a constant thing, no matter how much energy I have. I haven't really paid attention to whether my mood changes how strong it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your belief about people tracking your purchases might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not really. That's actually the main reason I ended up coming in here. I just can't shake the feeling that people at work, and even some of the vendors, are tracking my buys. I know they're messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. My supervisor has sat down with me and shown me the reports, and he says they don't prove anything is happening, but... I still know it's real. I just can't believe it's a mistake.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling extremely energetic and irritable, do you think your belief that people are tracking your purchases is more convincing to you than at other times?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say it's more convincing then. It's just... it's always convincing. Like I was saying, it doesn't really matter if I'm wired and irritable or if I'm just having a regular day. The belief is just there, and it always feels just as real. I don't think my energy level changes how much I believe it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt really down or depressed, and during that time, did you still believe people were tracking your purchases?\n\nPatient: I haven't really had a time where I felt super down or depressed. I mean, I get stressed out about the whole tracking thing, but I wouldn't call it depressed. So, I guess I can't really say if the belief would be there during a time like that, since I haven't really experienced it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree that you're being tracked if they knew all the facts you know?\n\nPatient: I think... yeah, if they really looked at it, they'd have to agree. Like I said before, that's what made me come in here in the first place. I just can't shake this feeling that everyone at work, and the vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. It's too obvious to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt really down or depressed for an extended period, and if so, did you still believe people were tracking your purchases during that time?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, I haven't really had any periods where I felt really down or depressed like that. I get stressed, sure, but not depressed.
